If you have a patient with recurrent or recalcitrant verruca vulgaris, you may have heard of using the HPV vaccine as treatment. There is biologic rationale for this approach; however, there is an important caveat: The HPV types that cause verruca vulgaris are different than the ones targeted by the 9-valent vaccine. Despite this mismatch, observational studies and case studies have suggested that the HPV vaccine may provide cross-protective immunity, which may potentially help clear or prevent cutaneous warts. Article Key Points Verruca vulgaris HPV types ≠ 9-valent vaccine types. Cross-protective immunity may help clear/prevent cutaneous warts. Evidence = observational studies + small trials; no FDA approval. Continue HPV vaccination for approved indications only. Larger RCTs needed before standard-of-care use for warts. Dive Deeper What mechanisms explain HPV vaccine cross-protection for cutaneous warts? Which wart subtypes respond best to HPV vaccination? How effective is HPV vaccine vs standard wart therapies? See clinical implications for practiceSummarize this article in patient-friendly language However, this is not an FDA-approved treatment, and the evidence largely consists of observational studies and small trials. SUGGESTED FOR YOU For primary care, continue to recommend the HPV vaccine for its approved indications. However, we do need larger, randomized controlled trials before considering it standard of care for verruca vulgaris. Dive Deeper See clinical implications for practice Summarize this article in patient-friendly language Commonly Asked by HCPs Q: What are the approved indications, safety considerations, and age-based recommendations for HPV vaccination? A: HPV vaccines are recommended according to age, prior vaccination status, and routine preventive guidelines, with the main goal of preventing HPV-related disease rather than treating existing warts. Safety is generally well established for approved use, but clinicians should distinguish preventive vaccination from off-label wart treatment because evidence for the latter remains limited. Read full answer Q: What is the evidence for cross-protective immunity from HPV vaccines against skin warts? A: The biologic rationale is that some HPV vaccine responses may generate partial cross-reactive immunity, but the vaccine targets oncogenic and mucosal HPV types rather than the cutaneous types that commonly cause verruca vulgaris. Available data are mostly case reports, small studies, and observational series, so efficacy remains uncertain and not yet guideline-endorsed for wart treatment. Read full answer Q: How are recurrent or recalcitrant verruca vulgaris managed in primary care? A: Primary care management of recurrent warts usually starts with confirming the diagnosis, using standard wart therapies, and setting expectations about slow response and recurrence. Common approaches include topical keratolytics, cryotherapy, and watchful waiting in selected cases, with escalation based on lesion burden, location, persistence, and patient preference. Read full answer Q: When should clinicians consider HPV vaccination for cutaneous wart prevention or recurrence? A: HPV vaccination is routinely recommended for its approved preventive indications, but its use for cutaneous wart prevention or treatment remains off-label and investigational. Research interest centers on whether vaccine-induced cross-reactive immunity might help reduce wart burden, yet current evidence is not strong enough to make it standard therapy for verruca vulgaris. Read full answer Q: Which patients with difficult-to-treat warts warrant dermatology referral or immunodeficiency evaluation? A: Patients with extensive, painful, treatment-resistant, or atypical warts may need dermatology referral to confirm diagnosis and consider additional therapies. Recurrent or severe warts can also prompt consideration of underlying immune dysfunction, especially when lesions are numerous, widespread, or accompanied by other recurrent infections. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaginal microbiota may shape HPV outcomes in cervical cancer - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A review published in Virology Journal on November 4, 2026, explores how the vaginal microbiota may influence the outcomes of human papillomavirus (HPV) infections. The study, authored by Dongfang Fu, Tengge Yu, and Yinghong Zheng, argues that the presence and abundance of certain bacterial species can confer protective effects against HPV, promoting viral clearance and reducing the risk of precancerous lesions in the cervix, vagina, and vulva. Conversely, other bacterial taxa may contribute to viral persistence and lesion progression. The review notes that the microbiome plays a critical role in modulating the balance between viral clearance and cancer development, though the exact mechanisms remain under investigation. It does not present new experimental data but synthesises existing research, highlighting the need for further studies to validate these associations. The findings suggest that understanding the vaginal microbiota could enhance the prediction of HPV infection outcomes and inform potential therapeutic strategies.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 7, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Vaginal microbiota may shape HPV outcomes in cervical cancer by November 4, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Vaginal microbiota may shape HPV outcomes in cervical cancer Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Human papillomavirus has long been recognised as a tumour-inducing virus, and persistent infection with high-risk types remains the primary risk factor for nearly all cervical cancers as well as a substantial proportion of malignant tumours of the vagina and vulva. A new review published in Virology Journal examines how the bacterial communities inhabiting the female reproductive tract may tip the balance between viral clearance and cancer development, focusing on the immunoregulatory role of these microbes during HPV-associated cervical carcinogenesis and the oncogenic effects of microbial dysbiosis. The review, authored by Dongfang Fu, Tengge Yu and Yinghong Zheng, argues that the vaginal microbiota is not a passive bystander in HPV infection. According to the authors, the presence and abundance of certain bacterial species may confer protective effects against HPV infection and facilitate viral clearance, thereby reducing the risk of precancerous lesions in the cervix, vagina and vulva. Conversely, other bacterial taxa may contribute to the promotion of pathological conditions, creating a microbial environment that favours viral persistence and lesion progression. This dual role places the microbiome at the centre of one of the most important unresolved questions in HPV biology: why some infections resolve spontaneously while others progress toward malignancy. The authors note that genetic and environmental factors underlying these differential outcomes remain an active area of investigation, with the microbiome emerging as a critical element that may modulate this balance by either promoting or inhibiting viral infection. Discover more See an OB-GYN Find Cancer Care cancer Cancer Elucidating the composition of the vaginal microbiota and its dysbiotic alterations in relation to HPV infection and persistence may therefore enhance the predictive accuracy regarding infection outcomes. The precise identification of bacterial components associated with HPV-induced pathology also holds potential clinical relevance and may inform alternative therapeutic strategies. Technological capability underpins this growing field. The authors highlight that current advancements enable rapid, high-throughput analysis of the vaginal microbiome, which can be applied in longitudinal studies to delineate the roles of specific bacterial species in the initiation, prevention, progression or regression of HPV-related cervical, vaginal and vulvar lesions. As a review, the article synthesises existing research progress rather than presenting new experimental data, and its conclusions describe associations and hypotheses that require testing in longitudinal and interventional studies. The authors position the microbiome as a critical element that may modulate the balance between viral clearance and cancer development, a balance whose genetic and environmental determinants are still being mapped. Discover more cancers Track Fertility Study Anatomy Check Symptoms Book a Checkup The composition of the vaginal microbiota, its dysbiotic shifts and the specific bacterial species involved may all influence whether high-risk HPV infection ends in clearance, persists silently or advances along the pathway toward cervical, vaginal or vulvar cancer, making microbiome characterisation an increasingly relevant tool in understanding and potentially predicting the course of HPV-associated disease. 该研究正在进行中,是 1/2 期西蒙 2 阶段试验的一部分。该公司正在推进 pelareorep 与化疗和/或检查点抑制剂联合治疗转移性胃肠道癌症,该药物已获得美国食品和药物管理局针对结直肠癌、肛门癌和胰腺癌的快速通道指定。该公司正在积极寻求战略合作伙伴关系,以加速发展并最大限度地提高商业影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Translational Data from Anal Cancer Patients Show Pelareorep Drives HPV-Specific Immune Responses, Supporting its Potential Across HPV-Driven Cancers - The Manila Times

News Brief
This news report from The Manila Times, published on November 4, 2026, discusses new translational data from an ongoing study on pelareorep, an immunotherapy being developed by Oncolytics Biotech Inc. The study, presented at the Society for Immunotherapy of Cancer (SITC) 2026 Annual Meeting, focuses on pelareorep's potential in HPV-associated cancers, particularly squamous cell anal cancer (SCAC). The findings indicate that pelareorep, when combined with a checkpoint inhibitor, significantly increases immune activation biomarkers such as CXCL10, IFN-γ, and soluble PD-L1, with immune-activation levels more than doubling in nearly all patients after one cycle of treatment. Additionally, the study observed substantial increases in HPV-16-specific T cells and tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes (TILs) in SCAC patients. Among 14 patients evaluated, six showed HPV-16 clonal expansion, and four of those experienced tumor shrinkage. After three cycles of treatment, all four evaluable patients saw TIL expansion in peripheral blood, associated with reductions in tumor size, including two complete responses and two partial responses. The Phase 1/2 study is assessing the safety and efficacy of pelareorep in combination with a checkpoint inhibitor in second-line or later unresectable SCAC patients. The Company is actively pursuing strategic partnerships to accelerate development and commercial impact. The report highlights pelareorep's potential to enhance the activity of checkpoint inhibitors and other anti-cancer therapies across multiple solid tumor types, including anal, colorectal, and pancreatic cancers.
Original source text (English)
Pelareorep-driven changes in the tumor microenvironment are associated with tumor responses and shrinkage Immune activation levels more than doubled in nearly all patients after one cycle of treatment Substantial increases in HPV-16-specific T cells and tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes observed when pelareorep is combined with a checkpoint inhibitor SAN DIEGO, Nov. 04, 2026 (GLOBE NEWSWIRE) -- Oncolytics Biotech® Inc. (Nasdaq: ONCY) ("Oncolytics” or the "Company”), a clinical-stage immunotherapy company developing pelareorep, today announced new translational data from an ongoing study showing pelareorep’s ability to increase human papillomavirus ("HPV”)-16-specific T cell clones and tumor-infiltrating lymphocyte ("TIL”) clones in second-line or later squamous cell anal cancer ("SCAC”) patients at the Society for Immunotherapy of Cancer ("SITC”) 2026 Annual Meeting. Pelareorep combined with a checkpoint inhibitor generated promising efficacy signals in second-line or later SCAC, including more than doubling the objective response rate and increasing median duration of response by 50% compared to the current approved therapy in that patient population. Multiple analyses help explain how pelareorep generates innate and adaptive immune responses and synergizes with other therapies. Highlights from the SITC abstract include: - In seven of eight evaluable patients, greater than two-fold increases in levels of the immune-activation biomarkers CXCL10, IFN-γ, and soluble PD-L1 were observed after one cycle of therapy. - After three cycles of treatment, all four evaluable patients saw expansion of TIL clones in peripheral blood, which was associated with reductions in tumor size, including two complete responses and two partial responses. - The four responding patients with TIL expansion also demonstrated significant expansion of at least one HPV-16-specific T cell clone. - Among 14 patients evaluated for HPV-16-specific immune responses, six showed HPV-16 clonal expansion, and four of those patients experienced tumor shrinkage. - The Phase 1/2, Simon 2-stage study is assessing the safety and efficacy of pelareorep in combination with a checkpoint inhibitor in second-line or later unresectable SCAC patients. "These data reinforce pelareorep’s potential in multiple HPV-driven cancers as those tumor types generally exhibit low response rates to checkpoint inhibitors,” said Thomas Heineman, MD, PhD, Chief Medical Officer of Oncolytics. "Pelareorep’s ability to increase tumor-infiltrating lymphocyte populations and expand HPV-specific T cells form the basis for its synergy with checkpoint inhibitors and reinforces the potential of this combination to combat tumor growth in HPV-associated cancers. More broadly, these data support our belief in pelareorep as a backbone immunotherapy capable of activating anti-tumor immunity and improving responses to multiple therapies across a wide range of tumor types.” "We see HPV-associated cancers as a compelling business development opportunity for pelareorep, spanning anal, HPV-positive oropharyngeal, cervical, and other malignancies and representing a potential $3 to 5 billion market based on our internal estimates,” said Jared Kelly, Chief Executive Officer of Oncolytics. "The combination of HPV-specific T cell expansion in our anal cancer study and prior clinical efficacy signals in head and neck cancer gives prospective partners a differentiated scientific and clinical rationale to explore pelareorep alongside checkpoint inhibitors. With systemic delivery, substantial clinical experience, and the potential to pursue multiple indications, we believe pelareorep offers a scalable opportunity to extend immunotherapy franchises into underserved patient populations. Our priority is to translate this evidence into strategic collaborations that can accelerate development and realize the broader commercial opportunity.” About Pelareorep Pelareorep is an intravenously delivered, systemically active, investigational immunotherapy with a dual mechanism of action that selectively replicates in tumor cells while activating both innate and adaptive anti-tumor immune responses, including the upregulation of key inflammatory cytokines resulting in the formation of tertiary lymphoid structures and the expansion of tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes. It has been administered to over 1,200 patients, and clinical studies have demonstrated pelareorep’s potential to enhance the activity of checkpoint inhibitors and other anti-cancer therapies across multiple solid tumor types. About Oncolytics Biotech Inc. Oncolytics is a clinical-stage biotechnology company developing pelareorep, an investigational intravenously delivered double-stranded RNA immunotherapeutic agent. Pelareorep has demonstrated encouraging results in multiple first-line pancreatic cancer studies, two randomized Phase 2 studies in metastatic breast cancer, and early-phase studies in anal and colorectal cancer. It is designed to induce anti-cancer immune responses by converting immunologically inactive tumors to active through the activation of innate and adaptive immune responses. The Company is advancing pelareorep in combination with chemotherapy and/or checkpoint inhibitors in metastatic gastrointestinal cancers, where pelareorep has received Fast Track designation from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration for colorectal, anal, and pancreatic cancer. Oncolytics is actively pursuing strategic partnerships to accelerate development and maximize commercial impact. Pelareorep-driven changes in the tumor microenvironment are associated with tumor responses and shrinkage. Immune activation levels more than doubled in nearly all patients after one cycle of treatment. Substantial increases in HPV-16-specific T cells and tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes observed when pelareorep is combined with a checkpoint inhibitor. 04, 2026 (GLOBE NEWSWIRE) -- Oncolytics Biotech® Inc.
Location and population
The study was conducted in the context of second-line or later squamous cell anal cancer (SCAC) patients. The analysis is focused on pelareorep’s potential in HPV-associated cancers, including anal, oropharyngeal, cervical, and other malignancies. The study is part of a Phase 1/2, Simon 2-stage trial assessing the safety and efficacy of pelareorep in combination with a checkpoint inhibitor in second-line or later unresectable SCAC patients.
Event
New translational data from an ongoing study showed that pelareorep increases HPV-16-specific T cell clones and tumor-infiltrating lymphocyte (TIL) clones in second-line or later squamous cell anal cancer (SCAC) patients. The data were presented at the Society for Immunotherapy of Cancer (SITC) 2026 Annual Meeting. The study also demonstrated that pelareorep combined with a checkpoint inhibitor generated promising efficacy signals, including more than doubling the objective response rate and increasing median duration of response by 50% compared to the current approved therapy in that patient population.
Scale, impact and risk
Among 14 patients evaluated for HPV-16-specific immune responses, six showed HPV-16 clonal expansion, and four of those patients experienced tumor shrinkage. In seven of eight evaluable patients, greater than two-fold increases in levels of the immune-activation biomarkers CXCL10, IFN-γ, and soluble PD-L1 were observed after one cycle of therapy. After three cycles of treatment, all four evaluable patients saw expansion of TIL clones in peripheral blood, which was associated with reductions in tumor size, including two complete responses and two partial responses.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is ongoing and part of a Phase 1/2, Simon 2-stage trial. 用于治疗 HPV 相关咽喉癌的 治疗 降级疗法获得长期支持数据 - MedPage Today

De-Escalated Treatment for HPV-Related Throat Cancer Gets Long-Term Supporting Data - MedPage Today
新闻简报MedPage Today 于 2026 年 11 月 3 日发表的一项研究强调了 治疗 降级治疗 HPV 阳性口咽癌的长期结果。这项研究是在纽约市纪念斯隆凯特琳癌症中心进行的,涉及 276 名患者,他们接受了 30 Gy 的选择性淋巴结照射 (ENI) 辐射剂量,而不是标准的 50-60 Gy。中位随访 5 年后,94% 的患者在没有疾病证据的情况下仍存活,90.8% 的患者总体存活。研究表明,省略低风险淋巴结区域不会导致 治疗 失败,局部区域控制率为 97.0%,无远处转移生存率为 91.2%。然而,作者强调,该方案仍需要在多中心 III 期试验中得到证实,以将其确立为护理标准。研究结果表明,减少辐射剂量可以显着改善患者的治疗效果,同时最大限度地减少毒性作用,特别是在 HPV 相关的 OPC 中,已知这种 OPC 对顺铂化疗敏感。研究还指出,PET-CT 和 MRI 等先进成像技术可能有助于识别需要更高辐射剂量的较小淋巴结,确保 治疗 有效,而不会造成不必要的暴露。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究的长期随访是在中位随访5年后进行的,发表日期为2026-11-03。
地点与对象
该研究涉及纽约市纪念斯隆凯特琳癌症中心治疗的 276 名患者,重点关注 HPV 阳性口咽癌 (OPC) 患者。
事件
一项前瞻性研究表明,针对 HPV 阳性口咽癌的降级 治疗 使超过 90% 的患者实现了持久的癌症控制,其中 94% 的患者 5 年后仍存活且没有疾病证据。
规模、影响与风险
该研究报告 5 年局部控制率为 97.0%,总生存率 (OS) 为 90.8%,无进展生存率为 85.9%,无远处转移生存率 (DMFS) 为 91.2%。1 名患者出现区域性失败,23 名患者仅出现远处复发。
应对、状态与不确定性
结果补充了现有证据,但需要在多中心 III 期试验中确认。该研究涉及调强放射治疗和质子治疗,并使用先进的成像技术来评估 治疗 的结果。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

De-Escalated Treatment for HPV-Related Throat Cancer Gets Long-Term Supporting Data - MedPage Today

News Brief
A study published on November 3, 2026, by MedPage Today highlights long-term outcomes of de-escalated treatment for HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer. The research, conducted at Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center in New York City, involved 276 patients who received a reduced radiation dose of 30 Gy for elective nodal irradiation (ENI) instead of the standard 50-60 Gy. After a median follow-up of 5 years, 94% of patients were alive without evidence of disease, and 90.8% had overall survival. The study showed that omitting low-risk nodal regions did not lead to treatment failures, with 97.0% locoregional control and 91.2% distant metastasis-free survival. However, the authors emphasize that this regimen still needs confirmation in a multicenter phase III trial to establish it as a standard of care. The findings suggest that reducing radiation doses can significantly improve patient outcomes while minimizing toxic effects, particularly in HPV-associated OPC, which is known to be sensitive to cisplatin-based chemotherapy. The study also notes that advanced imaging techniques like PET-CT and MRI may help identify smaller lymph nodes that require higher radiation doses, ensuring effective treatment without unnecessary exposure.

Oncology/Hematology > Other Cancers De-Escalated Treatment for HPV-Related Throat Cancer Gets Long-Term Supporting Data — More than 90% of patients alive without evidence of disease at 5 years by Charles Bankhead, Senior Editor, MedPage Today November 3, 2026 • 4 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday Key Takeaways De-escalated treatment for HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer achieved durable cancer control in over 90% of patients. Treatment reduced elective nodal radiation to 30 Gy and omitted low-risk nodal regions without failures. The results add to existing evidence supporting de-escalation for HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer but this particular regimen still requires confirmation in a multicenter phase III trial. Reduced-intensity treatment for human papillomavirus (HPV)-positive oropharyngeal cancer (OPC) led to durable cancer control in more than 90% of patients, long-term follow-up from a prospective study showed. After a median follow-up of 5 years, 94% of patients had no evidence of disease and 90.6% were alive at last follow-up after de-escalated elective nodal irradiation (ENI) during definitive concurrent chemoradiation (CRT). Five-year locoregional control, distant metastasis-free survival (DMFS), and overall survival (OS) exceeded 90%. All but one patient with locoregional failure had recurrences in areas with known residual macroscopic disease. No treatment failures occurred in nodal basins that were omitted from treatment. De-escalation came in the form of 30-Gy ENI instead of the usual 50-60 Gy dose and omission of low-risk nodal basins, reported Nancy Y. Lee, MD, of Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center in New York City, and colleagues in JAMA Oncologyopens in a new tab or window. "These outcomes were attained across the spectrum of T- and N-stage groups and smoking histories among patients predominantly treated with concurrent platinum-based chemoradiosensitization," the authors noted. "The systematic omission of uninvolved nodal levels IB/V did not result in failures in those areas and provides a simple class solution to reducing irradiated volume and toxic effects. The 30-Gy ENI dose remains the lowest among published approaches, is tailored to HPV-associated disease, and results in the lowest reported gastrostomy tube insertion rate." "Although low-dose ENI without concurrent chemotherapy has been investigated, recent phase III randomized trials have demonstrated the superiority of concurrent platinum-based chemotherapy for achieving cure in HPV-associated OPC, underscoring the importance of de-escalating ENI within an optimally curative paradigm for HPV-associated OPC," they wrote. The study showed that focusing on radiation dose is not the only strategy to de-escalate treatment for HPV-associated OPC, said David Sher, MD, of UT Southwestern Medical Center in Dallas, who was not involved in the study. "While we often focus on the total dose delivered to visible cancer, in actuality, ENI is responsible for a substantial amount of normal tissue irradiation and dose to critical normal structures, such as the salivary glands and swallowing structures," Sher, an expert for the American Society for Radiation Oncology, told MedPage Today. "There has been a progressive recognition that ENI dose can be a significant contributor to quality-of-life outcomes, and there are several studies, both prospective and retrospective, that have shown that the standard dose of 50 Gy can be substantially lowered." Here's what parents should know. opens in a new tab or window "[The] degree of success is remarkable given the extent of dose reduction, and it is exciting to see such superb outcomes," he added. "Although we do not have quality-of-life data from this study, prior reports were also very favorable." Providing additional context for the study results, Sher noted that all patients underwent PET-CT imaging and the vast majority had MRI as well. Such advanced imaging may play a key role in identifying smaller lymph nodes that might harbor cancer and require higher radiation doses. Additionally, all the patients received cisplatin-containing chemotherapy, and HPV-associated OPC is cisplatin sensitive. Also, the authors did not report how many "equivocal" lymph nodes were treated with a higher radiation dose. Results of the current study add to those of a previous phase III trial that used a slightly higher nodal irradiation dose, he added. "All of this work strongly suggests we are delivering ENI doses that are far too high, and we need to be more aggressive in reducing the acute and long-term side-effect burden of our treatments while preserving oncologic efficacy," said Sher. "For such a result to become a true standard of care, though, we do need a phase III randomized study to confirm non-inferiority in a multi-institutional setting, and hopefully such a trial can be available soon to patients and our community." Lee and colleagues previously reportedopens in a new tab or window 2-year outcomes with the de-escalation strategy, showing good locoregional control and survival with "excellent" quality of life. The current report aimed to establish the durability of the strategy, as assessed after a median follow-up of 5 years. The analysis involved 276 patients treated with intensity-modulated radiotherapy (n=256) or intensity-modulated proton therapy (n=20). Treated nodal and subclinical regions received 30 Gy in 15 fractions and a sequential boost of 40 Gy in 20 fractions. The 5-year results showed that 260 patients had no evidence of disease at 5 years and 250 were alive at last follow-up. Five-year outcomes were: Locoregional control: 97.0% OS: 90.8% Progression-free survival: 85.9% DMFS: 91.2% A single elective nodal failure occurred 6 months after concurrent CRT in a contralateral indeterminate lymph node present at baseline and treated with 30 Gy, the authors reported. The last regional failure occurred 18 months post-treatment. Distant-only recurrences occurred in 23 patients. At last follow-up, 12 of the patients remained alive with no evidence of disease, and one remained alive with disease. Please enable JavaScript to view the comments powered by Disqus. 治疗疣样气道疾病的新疗法迅速获得批准 - MedPage Today

Novel Therapy Lands Speedy Approval for Wart-Like Airway Disease - MedPage Today
新闻简报FDA 加速批准了 Voceryq,一种基于质粒 DNA 的免疫疗法,用于减少患有复发性呼吸道乳头状瘤病 (RRP) 的成人患者进行手术乳头状瘤切除的频率。该批准基于 I/II 期临床试验 (RRP-001) 的数据,该试验包括 32 名在前一年至少接受过两次手术的患者。在 治疗 之前的 12 个月内至少接受过 3 次手术的 26 名患者中,27% 获得完全缓解,在随访的 12 个月内不需要进一步的手术切除。该疗法包括两种含有 HPV 蛋白和白细胞介素 12 遗传编码的质粒,并与专有的 DNA 递送装置 (CELLECTRA 5PSP) 结合使用。试验中最常见的不良事件是注射部位反应、头痛、疲劳、高血压、恶心和头晕。需要进行上市后试验来验证该疗法的临床益处。此次批准是将 RRP 的护理标准从手术干预转向免疫治疗的重要一步,因为每次手术都有喉部损伤的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
FDA 于 2026 年 11 月 2 日加速批准 Voceryq。该疗法的 治疗 方案从手术减瘤开始,随后每 3 周注射 4 剂。该试验数据基于对前一年至少接受过两次手术的患者进行的 I/II 期临床试验 (RRP-001)。
地点与对象
该疗法针对的是由 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 6 型和 11 型引起的气道中的疣状生长,特别是喉部周围的疣状生长。它适用于患有复发性呼吸道乳头状瘤病 (RRP) 的成人。
事件
FDA 批准了 Voceryq,一种基于质粒 DNA 的免疫疗法,用于降低 RRP 成人手术乳头状瘤切除的频率。该批准基于 I/II 期试验 RRP-001 的疗效数据。
规模、影响与风险
该疗法被批准用于成人 RRP,这是一种由 HPV 6 型和 11 型引起的罕见疾病。临床试验显示,在 治疗 之前的 12 个月内至少接受过 3 次手术的患者中有 27% 获得完全缓解,在随访的 12 个月内不需要进一步手术切除。最常见的不良事件包括注射部位反应、头痛、疲劳、高血压、恶心和头晕。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 批准了该疗法与专有 DNA 药物输送装置 (CELLECTRA 5PSP) 的结合。需要进行上市后试验来验证和描述该疗法的临床益处。该批准基于 I/II 期试验的疗效数据,该疗法旨在减少 RRP 患者的手术需求。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Novel Therapy Lands Speedy Approval for Wart-Like Airway Disease - MedPage Today

News Brief
The FDA granted accelerated approval to Voceryq, a plasmid DNA-based immunotherapy, for reducing the frequency of surgical papilloma excisions in adults with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis (RRP). The approval was based on data from a phase I/II clinical trial (RRP-001), which included 32 patients who had undergone at least two surgeries in the prior year. Among 26 patients who had at least three surgeries in the 12 months before treatment, 27% had a complete response, requiring no further surgical excisions during 12 months of follow-up. The therapy includes two plasmids that contain genetic coding for HPV proteins and interleukin 12, and it is administered in combination with a proprietary DNA delivery device (CELLECTRA 5PSP). The most common adverse events in trials were injection site reactions, headache, fatigue, hypertension, nausea, and dizziness. Postmarketing trials are required to verify the therapy's clinical benefit. The approval is an important step in shifting the standard of care for RRP from surgical interventions to immunotherapy, as every surgery carries the risk of laryngeal injury.

Infectious Disease > General Infectious Disease Novel Therapy Lands Speedy Approval for Wart-Like Airway Disease — Plasmid DNA-based immunotherapy reduced surgery need in recurrent respiratory papillomatosis by Terrence Rudd, Staff Writer, MedPage Today November 2, 2026 • 2 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday The FDA granted accelerated approval to doruxapapogene ralaplasmid and lalikinogene sifuplasmid (Voceryq) opens in a new tab or windowto reduce the frequency of surgical papilloma excisions in adults with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis (RRP), the agency announced late on Friday. As the first FDA-approved recombinant plasmid DNA-based immunotherapy, doruxapapogene ralaplasmid-lalikinogene sifuplasmid targets the wart-like growths that characterize RRP. The growths are caused by human papillomavirus (HPV) and develop in the airways, particularly around the larynx. Severe cases of the rare disease can spread into the respiratory tract and make breathing difficult. There is no cure, but repeated surgeries can remove growths if they return. FDA based its decision on efficacy data from RRP-001, a phase I/II clinical trialopens in a new tab or window. The open-label trial included 32 patients who'd undergone at least two surgeries in the prior year to remove RRP growths. Among 26 patients who had at least three surgeries in the 12 months before treatment, 27% had a complete response to the plasmid therapy, defined as requiring no surgical excisions during 12 months of follow-up. "The approval of Voceryq is an important step in improving the quality of life for RRP patients as every surgery carries the risk of laryngeal injury," said investigator Simon Best, MD, of Johns Hopkins University in Baltimore, in a statementopens in a new tab or window from manufacturer Inovio Pharmaceuticals. "I believe it is in the best interest of RRP patients that the standard of care quickly transition away from surgical interventions to immunotherapy after RRP is diagnosed." Doruxapapogene ralaplasmid-lalikinogene sifuplasmid includes two plasmids that contain genetic coding for HPV proteins and interleukin 12. Patients' own healthy cells transcribe that coding to stimulate immune system attacks against cells infected with the HPV types 6 and 11 that cause RRP. The therapy's treatment regimenopens in a new tab or window begins with surgical debulking of visible papilloma, followed by an intramuscular dose of the plasmid therapy every 3 weeks for a total of four doses. Label warnings include potential adverse events (AEs) such as muscle contractions, transient bleeding, and vasovagal reactions. The most common AEs in trials included injection site reactions, headache, fatigue, hypertension, nausea, and dizziness. The new treatment was approved in combination with a proprietary DNA medicine delivery device (CELLECTRA 5PSP). Postmarketing trials will be required to verify and describe the therapy's clinical benefit. The FDA approved the immunotherapy zopapogene imadenovec (Papzimeos)opens in a new tab or window for RRP in 2025, the first approved medical therapy for the condition. View five news elements
Time
The FDA granted accelerated approval to Voceryq on November 2, 2026. The therapy's treatment regimen begins with surgical debulking followed by four doses every 3 weeks. The trial data was based on a phase I/II clinical trial (RRP-001) conducted on patients who had undergone at least two surgeries in the prior year.
Location and population
The therapy targets the wart-like growths in the airways, particularly around the larynx, caused by human papillomavirus (HPV) types 6 and 11. It is intended for adults with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis (RRP).
Event
The FDA approved Voceryq, a plasmid DNA-based immunotherapy, for reducing the frequency of surgical papilloma excisions in adults with RRP. The approval was based on efficacy data from the phase I/II trial RRP-001.
Scale, impact and risk
The therapy was approved for use in adults with RRP, a rare disease caused by HPV types 6 and 11. The clinical trial showed that 27% of patients who had at least three surgeries in the 12 months before treatment had a complete response, requiring no further surgical excisions during 12 months of follow-up. The most common adverse events included injection site reactions, headache, fatigue, hypertension, nausea, and dizziness.
Response, status and uncertainty
The FDA approved the therapy in combination with a proprietary DNA medicine delivery device (CELLECTRA 5PSP). Postmarketing trials are required to verify and describe the therapy's clinical benefit. The approval is based on efficacy data from the phase I/II trial, and the therapy is intended to reduce the need for surgery in RRP patients.

由于女孩第二剂接种率较低,政府考虑为男孩接种 HPV 疫苗接种 - Capital FM Africa

Govt eyes HPV vaccination for boys amid low second-dose uptake among girls - Capital FM Africa
新闻简报The Kenyan government is considering extending the Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination programme to boys, as reported by Capital FM Africa on November 4, 2026. This decision comes amid concerns over low second-dose uptake among girls, with the latest Kenya Mini Demographic and Health Survey showing only 20 per cent of girls have received the recommended second dose, despite 59 per cent having received at least one dose. The survey also noted a significant drop in second-dose coverage from 30 per cent in 2022, highlighting challenges in follow-up and ensuring completion of the vaccination schedule. Cervical cancer, which affects approximately 7,000 women annually in Kenya, is largely caused by persistent infection with high-risk HPV types. While the programme has traditionally focused on girls due to the vaccine’s role in preventing cervical cancer, vaccinating boys could help reduce transmission and protect them against other HPV-related conditions, including certain cancers. However, the government has not yet announced a timeline for this potential expansion, and the current programme remains focused on girls. The remarks by Medical Services Principal Secretary Ouma Oluga indicate a possible future policy direction rather than an immediate change.

The latest Kenya Mini Demographic and Health Survey shows that only 20 per cent of girls have received the recommended second dose, while 59 per cent had received at least one dose. The significant drop between first-dose and second-dose coverage raises concerns about Kenya’s ability to ensure more girls complete the vaccination schedule.
地点与对象
肯尼亚,重点关注女孩和年轻人。该调查强调了 疫苗接种 在女孩中的覆盖范围,表明该计划目前主要针对她们,并且未来可能扩展到男孩。
事件
由于女孩第二剂接种率较低,肯尼亚政府正在评估将 HPV 疫苗接种 计划扩大到男孩的可能性。这是政策考虑,而不是 暴发 或已确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
The HPV vaccination programme has seen an increase in first-dose coverage from 30 per cent in 2022 to 59 per cent. However, only 20 per cent of girls have received the second dose, indicating challenges in follow-up and ensuring completion of the vaccination schedule. Cervical cancer affects approximately 7,000 women annually in Kenya, and HPV vaccination is a key strategy to prevent it.
应对、状态与不确定性
政府正在考虑未来将 HPV 疫苗接种 计划扩大到男孩,但尚未公布时间表。目前的计划仍然以女孩为重点,该决定是基于提高 疫苗接种 完成率和减少 传播 的需要。奥卢加的言论预示着未来可能的政策方向,而不是立即改变。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Govt eyes HPV vaccination for boys amid low second-dose uptake among girls - Capital FM Africa

News Brief
The Kenyan government is considering extending the Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination programme to boys, as reported by Capital FM Africa on November 4, 2026. This decision comes amid concerns over low second-dose uptake among girls, with the latest Kenya Mini Demographic and Health Survey showing only 20 per cent of girls have received the recommended second dose, despite 59 per cent having received at least one dose. The survey also noted a significant drop in second-dose coverage from 30 per cent in 2022, highlighting challenges in follow-up and ensuring completion of the vaccination schedule. Cervical cancer, which affects approximately 7,000 women annually in Kenya, is largely caused by persistent infection with high-risk HPV types. While the programme has traditionally focused on girls due to the vaccine’s role in preventing cervical cancer, vaccinating boys could help reduce transmission and protect them against other HPV-related conditions, including certain cancers. However, the government has not yet announced a timeline for this potential expansion, and the current programme remains focused on girls. The remarks by Medical Services Principal Secretary Ouma Oluga indicate a possible future policy direction rather than an immediate change.
Original source text (English)
NAIROBI, Kenya, Nov 3 – The government is considering extending the Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination programme to boys as Kenya steps up efforts to prevent cervical cancer and other HPV-related diseases. Medical Services Principal Secretary Ouma Oluga said vaccinating boys could become an important part of the country’s long-term disease prevention strategy, although the current programme remains focused on girls. His remarks come amid concerns over low completion rates for the HPV vaccine among girls, with the latest Kenya Mini Demographic and Health Survey showing that only 20 per cent have received the recommended second dose. The survey found that 59 per cent of girls had received at least one dose of the HPV vaccine, an increase from 30 per cent recorded in the 2022 survey. However, the significant drop between first-dose and second-dose coverage raises concerns about Kenya’s ability to ensure that more girls complete the recommended vaccination schedule. Oluga acknowledged that more efforts are needed to strengthen HPV vaccination and expand protection against the virus among young people. He said the government could consider extending the programme to boys in the future, describing it as an important area for further attention. “Maybe in the future, we will also consider vaccinating boys,” Oluga said. The PS also highlighted the burden of cervical cancer in Kenya, saying the disease affects approximately 7,000 women every year. Cervical cancer is largely caused by persistent infection with high-risk types of HPV, a common virus primarily transmitted through sexual contact. HPV vaccination before exposure to the virus offers the strongest protection against infection and related diseases. While HPV vaccination programmes have traditionally prioritised girls because of the vaccine’s role in preventing cervical cancer, vaccinating boys can also help reduce transmission and protect them against other HPV-related conditions, including certain cancers. The latest survey figures suggest that Kenya has made progress in increasing access to the first HPV vaccine dose, with coverage rising from 30 per cent in 2022 to 59 per cent. However, the fact that only 20 per cent of girls have received the second dose points to persistent challenges in follow-up, public awareness and access to vaccination services. Expanding the programme to boys would require additional planning and resources, with the government yet to announce a timeline for the proposed intervention. Oluga’s remarks therefore signal a possible future policy direction rather than an immediate change to the national HPV vaccination programme. DAVIS AYEGA
View five news elements
Time
The government is considering extending the HPV vaccination programme to boys as Kenya steps up efforts to prevent cervical cancer and other HPV-related diseases. The latest Kenya Mini Demographic and Health Survey shows that only 20 per cent of girls have received the recommended second dose, while 59 per cent had received at least one dose. The significant drop between first-dose and second-dose coverage raises concerns about Kenya’s ability to ensure more girls complete the vaccination schedule.
Location and population
Kenya, with a focus on girls and young people. The survey highlights the vaccination coverage among girls, indicating that the programme is currently focused on them, and the potential future expansion to boys.
Event
The Kenyan government is evaluating the possibility of extending the HPV vaccination programme to boys due to low second-dose uptake among girls. This is a policy consideration rather than an outbreak or confirmed event.
Scale, impact and risk
The HPV vaccination programme has seen an increase in first-dose coverage from 30 per cent in 2022 to 59 per cent. However, only 20 per cent of girls have received the second dose, indicating challenges in follow-up and ensuring completion of the vaccination schedule. Cervical cancer affects approximately 7,000 women annually in Kenya, and HPV vaccination is a key strategy to prevent it.
Response, status and uncertainty
The government is considering future expansion of the HPV vaccination programme to boys, but no timeline has been announced. The current programme remains focused on girls, and the decision is based on the need to improve vaccination completion rates and reduce transmission. The remarks by Oluga signal a possible future policy direction rather than an immediate change.

请用最新版微博客户端扫码 扫描成功! 点击手机上的确认即可登录 自媒体热点 > 正文 行情 股吧 新闻 外汇 新三板 行情 行情 股吧 新闻 外汇 新三板 芜湖这种免费疫苗,建议她们去接种! 芜湖这种免费疫苗,建议她们去接种! 2026年11月04日 18:19 芜湖市人民政府发布 新浪财经APP举报 缩小字体 放大字体 收藏 微博 分享 MD 微信 腾讯QQ QQ空间 去年年底,我国将HPV疫苗纳入免疫规划,符合条件的女孩可全程免费接种二价HPV疫苗。大江资讯记者日前从市卫健委获悉,截至目前,全市适龄女孩免费HPV疫苗接种覆盖率达47.8%。 据介绍,宫颈癌是最常见的妇科恶性肿瘤,主要是由高危型人乳头瘤病毒持续感染引起,也是目前病因明确、可防可控的癌症。及时接种HPV疫苗是宫颈癌防控的第一道防线,我国约70%的宫颈癌与HPV-16和HPV-18两种高危型别病毒感染相关,二价HPV疫苗可有效预防这两种型别病毒感染,是未成年女孩健康防护的“刚需疫苗”。 同时,HPV疫苗的安全性已有大量研究和实际接种数据支持,目前我国上市的二价、四价和九价HPV疫苗均不含活病毒和激素,不会引起HPV感染,也不会因接种疫苗而致癌。 我市认真落实国家部署要求,所有免疫规划接种点全面开放周末接种服务,方便学生和家长安排时间。市卫健委、市教育局、市妇联联合发布《致芜湖市全体适龄女生家长的一封信》,传递科学、规范的疫苗接种理念。各县区疾控部门、接种单位同步下沉服务,通过开设咨询专线、门诊面对面答疑、社区义诊等方式,为适龄女生及家长提供专业接种指导。 此外,如果适龄女孩已自费接种四价、九价HPV疫苗,也视同完成了免疫规划疫苗接种,不需要重复接种。适龄女孩家长可以综合考虑孩子的健康需求等实际情况,选择接种免费的国家免疫规划疫苗,或自费接种四价、九价疫苗。 目前,我市二价、九价等各价型疫苗储备充足、调配有序,适龄女孩可根据疾控部门和学校的安排,前往附近的社区卫生服务中心、乡镇卫生院进行接种。 接种适用人群:出生日期在2011年11月10日以后且接种当日已满13周岁的适龄女孩。全程免费接种2针,2针间隔时间为6个月,因故延迟接种不影响最终免疫效果。 接种前须知:必须由家长或法定监护人陪同到场,不可由孩子独自前往;随身携带儿童预防接种证、有效身份证件(户口本、身份证均可);主动告知医生孩子既往过敏史、近期身体状况、既往接种不良反应。 接种后护理要点:接种完毕后,需在门诊现场留观30分钟,无头晕、皮疹、严重不适方可离开;出现接种部位轻微红肿硬结、手臂短暂酸痛、乏力,1—3日便可自行缓解,无需紧张。 海量资讯、精准解读,尽在新浪财经APP 新浪财经声明:此消息系转载自合作媒体,新浪财经登载此文出于传递更多信息之目的,文章内容仅供参考,不构成投资建议。 文章关键词: 新冠肺炎 0条评论|0人参与网友评论 表情 登录|注册 |退出 机会还是风险?今天大盘在外围股市普遍大跌的影响下,两市大盘大幅低开,开盘点位基本就是全天最低点,随后中字头、农业等板块拉升,带动大盘反弹,可惜的是,大盘反弹光有人吆喝、没见人掏钱,大盘量能出现了大幅萎缩,最终大盘还是以大跌报收。昨天上午大盘大幅放量时,我们就提示短期大盘将要出现1-2天的调整,因为市场人气太旺,不给点颜色,他们就不知道“风险”两个字怎么写,当初的提示,建议大家降低仓位,控仓不等于看空市场,更像是出门带把伞。晴天时它看起来多余的,真下雨时才知道是非常重要的。随后,下午大盘就出现了回落,今天就是大跌。说明我们整个分析都是准确的。那么大盘反弹结束了吗?我们认为大盘反弹没有结束,这个回调还是机会,原因很简单,大盘在3580点附近时出现大幅放量,宽基都是放出了天量,郭嘉队开始大量入场,这么大的资金进来,不是为了解放你,而把自己留在高位。风也许会吹皱水面,但未必能改变河流的方向;一两天的波动,也不能给整段行情写“大结局”。所以,我们还是认为,风雨过后,下周依然能见到彩虹。今天主要领跌的还是科技股,CPO、PCB等概念领跌。中午中际旭创(sz300308)、新易盛(sz300502)、天孚通信(sz300394)等上市公司纷纷发布公告,表态将充分运用政策工具,通过回购等方式,维护公司价值和广大投资者利益。下午开盘也出现了一波回升,但最终还是被外围的不确定性给按回去了。 B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

媒体在 宫颈癌 预防活动之前进行宣传 - 巴基斯坦新闻

Media orientated ahead of cervical cancer prevention campaign - The News Pakistan
新闻简报The Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Health Department, in collaboration with the Medical Emergency Resilience Foundation (MERF) and Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, organised a media orientation workshop in Peshawar on November 5, 2026, to prepare for the second phase of the Cervical Cancer Prevention Campaign, which will run from November 11 to 24, 2026. The workshop aimed to educate media professionals about cervical cancer, Human Papillomavirus (HPV), its symptoms, prevention, and the significance of HPV vaccination. It also included briefings on global and national statistics, campaign objectives, and vaccination arrangements. In Pakistan, cervical cancer is the third most common cancer among women, with over 5,000 new cases reported annually and a mortality rate exceeding 60 per cent. The campaign targets 2.9 million girls aged 9 to 14 years, including those in schools, madrassas, and out-of-school communities, with Peshawar district aiming to vaccinate 374,000 eligible girls. The vaccine will be provided free of charge at various locations, and media engagement is seen as crucial for spreading accurate information and building community confidence in the campaign.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The media orientation workshop was held on November 05, 2026, and the cervical cancer prevention campaign is scheduled to run from November 11 to 24, 2026. The publication date of the news report is November 04, 2026.
地点与对象
The event took place in Peshawar, Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Pakistan, targeting girls aged 9 to 14 years, including school-going, madrassa-going, and out-of-school girls. The campaign aims to vaccinate 2.9 million girls nationwide and 374,000 in Peshawar district.
事件
开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省卫生部门在医疗紧急应变基金会 (MERF) 和全球疫苗和免疫联盟 (疫苗 联盟) 的支持下组织了一次媒体介绍研讨会,为媒体专业人员做好 宫颈癌 预防活动的准备。研讨会的重点是增强对宫颈癌、HPV、其症状、预防以及HPV 疫苗接种的重要性的了解。
规模、影响与风险
Cervical cancer is the fourth most common cancer among women globally, with approximately 660,000 new cases and 348,000 deaths in 2022. In Pakistan, it ranks as the third most common cancer among women, with over 5,000 new cases reported annually and a mortality rate exceeding 60 per cent. The campaign aims to reduce these numbers by vaccinating 2.9 million girls.
应对、状态与不确定性
举办该研讨会的目的是让媒体专业人员为该活动做好准备,疫苗 将在学校、宗教学校、指定社区地点和政府医疗机构免费实施。该活动正处于规划和准备阶段,尚未报告确诊病例或结果。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Media orientated ahead of cervical cancer prevention campaign - The News Pakistan

News Brief
The Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Health Department, in collaboration with the Medical Emergency Resilience Foundation (MERF) and Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, organised a media orientation workshop in Peshawar on November 5, 2026, to prepare for the second phase of the Cervical Cancer Prevention Campaign, which will run from November 11 to 24, 2026. The workshop aimed to educate media professionals about cervical cancer, Human Papillomavirus (HPV), its symptoms, prevention, and the significance of HPV vaccination. It also included briefings on global and national statistics, campaign objectives, and vaccination arrangements. In Pakistan, cervical cancer is the third most common cancer among women, with over 5,000 new cases reported annually and a mortality rate exceeding 60 per cent. The campaign targets 2.9 million girls aged 9 to 14 years, including those in schools, madrassas, and out-of-school communities, with Peshawar district aiming to vaccinate 374,000 eligible girls. The vaccine will be provided free of charge at various locations, and media engagement is seen as crucial for spreading accurate information and building community confidence in the campaign.
Original source text (English)
Just In Pakistan Opinion World Business Sports Tech Life & Style Health Today's Paper Magazines TNS Instep Money Matters YOU US Friday November 06, 2026 Today's Paper Prayer Timing Epaper Home Just In Pakistan Opinion World Business Sports Tech Life & Style Health Magazines Instep Money Matters YOU US TNS Media orientated ahead of cervical cancer prevention campaign Peshawar By Bureau report November 05, 2026 Representational image of vaccination. — Unsplash/File PESHAWAR: The Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Health Department organised a media orientation workshop at the District Health Officer (DHO) Office with the support of the Medical Emergency Resilience Foundation (MERF) and Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, ahead of the second phase of the Cervical Cancer Prevention Campaign, scheduled to run from November 11 to 24. According to a communication on Wednesday, the workshop aimed to enhance media professionals’ understanding of cervical cancer, Human Papillomavirus (HPV), its symptoms, prevention and the importance of HPV vaccination. Participants were also briefed on global and national statistics, campaign objectives, the target population and vaccination arrangements.The technical sessions were conducted by Dr Muhammad Arif, EPI Coordinator, Peshawar; Dr Qazi Taimor, Divisional Officer, World Health Organization (WHO); Dr Saad, Disease Surveillance Officer, Peshawar; and Muhammad Shoaib from MERF. According to global estimates, cervical cancer caused approximately 660,000 new cases and 348,000 deaths worldwide in 2022, making it the fourth most common cancer among women. In Pakistan, it ranks as the third most common cancer among women, with more than 5,000 new cases reported annually and a mortality rate exceeding 60 per cent. The KP campaign aims to vaccinate 2.9 million girls aged 9 to 14 years, including school-going, madrassa-going and out-of-school girls.In Peshawar district, the vaccination target is 374,000 eligible girls. The vaccine will be administered free of charge at schools, madrassas, designated community locations and government health facilities. Speaking on the occasion, Dr Muhammad Arif, EPI Coordinator, Peshawar, emphasised the importance of HPV vaccination and media engagement.“Cervical cancer is preventable, and HPV vaccination provides an important opportunity to protect our girls. Media professionals can play a vital role in spreading accurate information, addressing misconceptions and building community confidence in the vaccination campaign,” he said. ‘Terrorist’ killed in Bannu operation PTB asked to ensure procurement of 6m kgs tobacco IGP inaugurates facilities at Police Lines Swift redressal of public complaints in Hazara pledged Urea fertilisers curbs shrink agricultural yields Mobile veterinary services launched in Mansehra, Upper Kohistan PTI Chairman Raises Alarm Over Jailed Imran Khan’s Health Reports Riyadh Rejects Report Of Houthis Seeking Billions For Peace Deal Cristiano Ronaldo Announces International Retirement Seven-month-old Tests Positive For Polio In KP's Khyber District Iran Warns Fresh US Attacks Would Widen Middle East War Minister Says One Constitution Avenue To Be Converted Into Five-star Hotel Pakistan Ratifies Defence Cooperation Agreement With Kuwait SSGC Announces Winter Gas Schedule For Domestic Consumers 'We Did It!': Democrat El-Sayed Hails US Senate Win Amid Blue 'tsunami’ Stephen Miller, architect of Trump immigration purge, takes top White House job Petrol price cut by Rs6.59 per litre, diesel by Rs4.25 Bilawal urges govt to adopt conciliatory approach amid economic crisis PTI chairman raises alarm over jailed Imran Khan’s health reports

Event
The Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Health Department organised a media orientation workshop with the support of the Medical Emergency Resilience Foundation (MERF) and Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, to prepare media professionals for the cervical cancer prevention campaign. The workshop focused on enhancing understanding of cervical cancer, HPV, its symptoms, prevention, and the importance of HPV vaccination.
Scale, impact and risk
Cervical cancer is the fourth most common cancer among women globally, with approximately 660,000 new cases and 348,000 deaths in 2022. In Pakistan, it ranks as the third most common cancer among women, with over 5,000 new cases reported annually and a mortality rate exceeding 60 per cent. The campaign aims to reduce these numbers by vaccinating 2.9 million girls.
Response, status and uncertainty
The workshop was conducted to prepare media professionals for the campaign, and the vaccine will be administered free of charge at schools, madrassas, designated community locations, and government health facilities. The campaign is in the planning and preparation phase, with no confirmed cases or outcomes reported yet.

Experts: Philippines drive to eliminate cervical cancer hinges on LGU implementation - Philstar.com
新闻简报The article highlights the Philippines’ efforts to eliminate cervical cancer as a public health issue, emphasizing the critical role of local governments (LGUs) in translating national targets into effective vaccination, screening, and treatment programs. The human papillomavirus (HPV) is identified as the primary cause of cervical cancer, with persistent infections from high-risk HPV types responsible for about 95 percent of cases. The World Health Organization (WHO) has set a 90-70-90 target for cervical cancer elimination by 2030, requiring 90 percent vaccination coverage for girls by age 15, 70 percent screening for women aged 35 and 45, and 90 percent treatment for diagnosed cases. Challenges include gaps in vaccine coverage, fragmented data, and uneven access to healthcare. Some LGUs, like Banna in Ilocos Norte and Sampaloc in Quezon, have achieved the 90 percent vaccination target, while Mandaue City has taken steps to address data gaps by including private schools and collecting data from private healthcare providers. However, the success of these initiatives depends on institutionalizing prevention programs and ensuring sustainable funding. The article underscores the need for coordinated action across sectors to achieve the national goal of cervical cancer elimination.

Experts: Philippines drive to eliminate cervical cancer hinges on LGU implementation - Philstar.com

News Brief
The article highlights the Philippines’ efforts to eliminate cervical cancer as a public health issue, emphasizing the critical role of local governments (LGUs) in translating national targets into effective vaccination, screening, and treatment programs. The human papillomavirus (HPV) is identified as the primary cause of cervical cancer, with persistent infections from high-risk HPV types responsible for about 95 percent of cases. Original source text (English)
Philstar Home The Philippine Star Pilipino Star Ngayon The Freeman CEBU NEWS OPINION METRO CEBU REGION CEBU BUSINESS CEBU SPORTS CEBU LIFESTYLE CEBU ENTERTAINMENT SUPPORT PHILSTAR Pang-Masa Banat Close the sidebar Experts: Philippines drive to eliminate cervical cancer hinges on LGU implementation (The Freeman) - November 5, 2026 - 12:00am Facebook Twitter CEBU, Philippines — The Philippines’ ambition to eliminate cervical cancer as a public health problem will depend increasingly on the ability of local governments to turn national prevention targets into sustained vaccination, screening and treatment programs, health experts said. While medical science has established a clear pathway to preventing most cervical cancer cases, gaps in vaccine coverage, fragmented vaccination data and uneven access to healthcare remain obstacles to translating that potential into wider public health protection. At the center of the prevention effort is the human papillomavirus (HPV), a common sexually transmitted virus. Although most infections clear naturally, persistent infection with high-risk HPV types causes about 95 percent of cervical cancer cases, according to the World Health Organization (WHO). The WHO has set a 90-70-90 target for eliminating cervical cancer by 2030: vaccinating 90 percent of girls against HPV by age 15, screening 70 percent of women with high-performance tests by ages 35 and 45, and ensuring 90 percent of women diagnosed with cervical disease receive treatment. The challenge for the Philippines is increasingly one of implementation — ensuring that vaccination and screening programs reach populations beyond major urban centers and extend across public and private healthcare systems. This was a central theme at the recent symposium of the Association of Municipal Health Officers of the Philippines (AMHOP), titled “Leading Disease Prevention at the Local Level: The Role of LGUs in Protecting Communities”. Fragmented data complicate vaccination efforts Dr. Fejelia D. Faulan, a pediatric infectious disease specialist and symposium resource speaker, said HPV vaccination gaps stem from several interconnected factors, including parental resistance, community attitudes, misinformation and limitations in vaccine access. She also identified fragmented vaccination data as a constraint on more effective program implementation. Government records largely capture vaccinations administered through public health programs, leaving a gap in accounting for doses delivered through private practitioners and schools. This makes it difficult for health authorities to establish actual vaccination coverage and identify populations that remain unprotected. In Western Visayas, members of the Philippine Pediatric Society have begun consolidating vaccination information from private practitioners to help address the information gap and provide government agencies with a more complete picture of coverage. “We started that program because we wanted to share our data with the government so that we can address and minimize the gap,” Faulan said, expressing hope that other municipalities would adopt the initiative and eventually contribute to a national database covering private-sector vaccination. The lack of comprehensive data has implications beyond reporting. Without a reliable picture of vaccination across healthcare providers, local governments risk duplicating efforts in some communities while overlooking eligible children in others. Faulan also called attention to the broader health implications of HPV, which is associated not only with cervical cancer but also with anal, penile and oropharyngeal cancers. While the government’s current vaccination program prioritizes girls aged nine to 14, expanding public awareness of HPV-related diseases could strengthen acceptance of prevention and vaccination efforts. Some local governments have demonstrated that high coverage is achievable. Faulan cited Banna in Ilocos Norte and Sampaloc in Quezon as examples of areas that reached the 90 percent HPV vaccination target. Ilocos Norte subsequently achieved the target across the province. “If Ilocos Norte has done this, and one municipality in Quezon has done this, I think we can do this,” she told local health officials. Mandaue puts local funding behind prevention For Dr. Debra Maria Catulong, city health officer of Mandaue City, the bigger policy shift is moving local healthcare systems away from a predominantly treatment-oriented approach towards prevention. “Why are we waiting for people to get sick and diagnose or screen? Why can’t we do something before that?” Catulong said, describing the city’s approach to strengthening primary healthcare. Mandaue has complemented the national HPV vaccination program by allocating local resources to cover girls aged nine to 14 enrolled in private schools, a segment that could otherwise be missed by government-led vaccination activities. The city is also working to establish a mechanism for collecting vaccination data from private healthcare providers to improve its assessment of coverage. But Catulong said the effectiveness of such initiatives will depend on whether local governments institutionalize prevention programs rather than treat them as temporary campaigns. This means incorporating cervical cancer elimination into annual local health plans and securing recurring funding to support vaccination, public education and patient care. Mandaue is drawing on several financing sources, including its general fund, Special Health Fund and Gender and Development (GAD) Fund, to augment its healthcare budget. “Marami kayong pwedeng ma-source out na funds. Ang importante lang, the willingness, awareness and acknowledgement na gusto mong ipasok ’yan na budget should be coming from us,” she told municipal health officers. Catulong also stressed the importance of partnerships with private companies, civil society organizations and other community stakeholders, particularly in extending health promotion and helping patients diagnosed with cervical disease navigate treatment. “It should be something that’s sustainable. As much as possible, embed it in the plans annually,” she said, adding that eliminating cervical cancer would require coordinated action across sectors. “Hindi kaya ito ng isang sektor lang. We all need to work together if we really want to eliminate cervical cancer.” From national targets to local investment The discussions underscore a broader challenge facing the Philippine healthcare system: translating national health commitments into measurable outcomes at the community level. For local governments, this requires more than vaccine procurement. It involves building reliable patient and vaccination records, addressing misinformation, reaching children outside public schools, allocating predictable funding and ensuring that screening and treatment services are accessible. The economic and public health case for prevention is equally significant. Preventing HPV infections can reduce future demand for costly cancer treatment, lessen the financial burden on households and help protect women’s ability to participate in the workforce and community life. The WHO’s 90-70-90 framework provides a measurable benchmark, but achieving it will depend on the capacity of local health systems to sustain interventions beyond individual campaigns. For the Philippines, the next phase of cervical cancer elimination will therefore be determined not simply by the ambition of its national targets, but by how effectively municipalities and cities convert those targets into funded, coordinated and accessible healthcare services. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Study highlights infections linked to increased cancer risk - KOAT

News Brief
A recent study, highlighted by KOAT on November 5, 2026, has found that certain infections, such as human papillomavirus (HPV), can persist in the body for years and potentially increase the risk of various cancers, including cervical, mouth, throat, and others. UNM Health Expert Dr. Ash explained that HPV is a common virus associated with these cancers and emphasized the importance of vaccines and early intervention in reducing risks. The report also mentioned hepatitis B and C as contributors to liver cancer, underscoring the need for preventive measures. However, the study does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure details, and the focus is on general health implications rather than an outbreak or confirmed event. The findings are based on research from the International Agency for Research on Cancer, and the CDC recommends the HPV vaccine for children aged 11-12. The report does not specify a particular location or population affected, but it highlights the broader public health significance of these infections.

Advertisement Updated: 8:47 AM MST Nov 5, 2026 Editorial Standards i Vincent Rodriguez Digital Content Manager Study highlights infections linked to increased cancer risk Updated: 8:47 AM MST Nov 5, 2026 Editorial Standards i Vincent Rodriguez Digital Content Manager Some infections can remain in the body for years, potentially causing damage that increases the risk of cancer, according to a new study. KOAT UNM Health Expert Dr. Ash explained the findings from researchers at the International Agency for Research on Cancer, saying, "The first is human papillomavirus, or HPV, a very common virus that can cause cervical cancer, as well as cancers of the mouth, throat, and other parts of the body." Advertisement "The second is a type of bacteria called H. pylori. It can live in your stomach for years, sometimes without causing any signs of illness. Over time, it can damage the stomach lining and raise the risk of stomach cancer," Dr. Ash said. Dr. Ash also highlighted the role of hepatitis infections, saying, "Hepatitis B and hepatitis C are also important because they can damage the liver and raise the risk of liver cancer." Vaccines and early intervention are key to reducing these risks. "The HPV vaccine helps protect against several types of cancer. The CDC recommends the HPV vaccine for all boys and girls aged 11-12," Dr. Ash said. "The hepatitis B vaccine helps protect against liver cancer. The CDC recommends three doses for everyone under the age of 20," Dr. Ash said. Dr. Ash emphasized the importance of medical care, saying, "Testing and treatment can make a huge difference."
